# DuckDuckGo Search MCP Server

[](#duckduckgo-search-mcp-server)

[![PyPI version](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/16ce668dfa63186428c148c2cf27d2eba717597411bf784247118791b5334992/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f707970692f762f6475636b6475636b676f2d6d63702d736572766572)](https://pypi.org/project/duckduckgo-mcp-server/) [![PyPI downloads](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/6e0ad7c5989409ffee31ebdd0c6bbc0ca38815c871c4b522af168dd9cf24161d/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f707970692f646d2f6475636b6475636b676f2d6d63702d736572766572)](https://pypi.org/project/duckduckgo-mcp-server/) [![Python versions](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/bd75ff55db07b44d4169e24ccc24e2bf497f02873dea797b9a3fb48fc752dc44/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f707970692f707976657273696f6e732f6475636b6475636b676f2d6d63702d736572766572)](https://pypi.org/project/duckduckgo-mcp-server/)

A Model Context Protocol (MCP) server that provides web search capabilities through DuckDuckGo, with additional features for content fetching and parsing.

## Quick Start

[](#quick-start)

```
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server
```

## Features

[](#features)

- **Web Search**: Search DuckDuckGo with advanced rate limiting and result formatting

- **Content Fetching**: Retrieve and parse webpage content with intelligent text extraction

- **Long URL Shortening**: Over-long result URLs become short `ref://` tokens that `fetch_content` accepts directly, saving context

- **Rate Limiting**: Built-in protection against rate limits for both search and content fetching

- **Error Handling**: Comprehensive error handling and logging

- **LLM-Friendly Output**: Results formatted specifically for large language model consumption

## Installation

[](#installation)

Install from PyPI using `uv`:

```
uv pip install duckduckgo-mcp-server
```

## Usage

[](#usage)

### Running with Claude Desktop

[](#running-with-claude-desktop)

1. Download [Claude Desktop](https://claude.ai/download)

2. Create or edit your Claude Desktop configuration:

   - On macOS: `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json`

   - On Windows: `%APPDATA%\Claude\claude_desktop_config.json`

Add the following configuration:

**Basic Configuration (No SafeSearch, No Default Region):**

```
{
    "mcpServers": {
        "ddg-search": {
            "command": "uvx",
            "args": ["duckduckgo-mcp-server"]
        }
    }
}
```

**With SafeSearch and Region Configuration:**

```
{
    "mcpServers": {
        "ddg-search": {
            "command": "uvx",
            "args": ["duckduckgo-mcp-server"],
            "env": {
                "DDG_SAFE_SEARCH": "STRICT",
                "DDG_REGION": "cn-zh"
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**Configuration Options:**

- `DDG_SAFE_SEARCH`: SafeSearch filtering level (optional)

  - `STRICT`: Maximum content filtering (kp=1)

  - `MODERATE`: Balanced filtering (kp=-1, default if not specified)

  - `OFF`: No content filtering (kp=-2)

- `DDG_REGION`: Default region/language code (optional, examples below)

  - `us-en`: United States (English)

  - `cn-zh`: China (Chinese)

  - `jp-ja`: Japan (Japanese)

  - `wt-wt`: No specific region

  - Leave empty for DuckDuckGo's default behavior

- `DDG_CA_CERTS`: Path to a PEM CA bundle used to verify TLS certificates on outbound requests (optional). Needed behind TLS-intercepting proxies — see [Running behind a TLS-intercepting proxy](#running-behind-a-tls-intercepting-proxy).

- `DDG_RATE_LIMIT_STRATEGY`: `sliding` (default, historical 60s window) or `token_bucket` (burst, then smooth).

- `DDG_SEARCH_RPM`: Search requests per minute (default: `30`).

- `DDG_FETCH_RPM`: Global `fetch_content` requests per minute (default: `20`).

- `DDG_FETCH_HOST_RPM`: Optional per-host fetch cap (default: `0`, off). Set a positive number to enable.

- `DDG_CACHE_TTL`: Seconds to keep a parsed page in the in-memory `fetch_content` cache (default: `300`). Paginated reads of the same URL reuse one download. Set `0` to disable.

- `DDG_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES`: Maximum pages kept in that cache (default: `64`). Least-recently-used eviction. Set `0` to disable.

- `DDG_PARSE_MODE`: Default `fetch_content` extractor (`text`, `main`, or `markdown`). Default is `text` (historical flattened page). Per-call `parse_mode` overrides this.

- `DDG_REF_URL_THRESHOLD`: Search-result URLs longer than this many characters are replaced with short `ref://<id>` tokens (default: `120`). Set `0` to always show full URLs. Also `--ref-url-threshold`.

3. Restart Claude Desktop

### Running with Claude Code

[](#running-with-claude-code)

1. Download [Claude Code](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code)

2. Ensure [`uvenv`](https://github.com/robinvandernoord/uvenv) is installed and the `uvx` command is available

3. Add the MCP server: `claude mcp add ddg-search uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server`

### Running with SSE or Streamable HTTP

[](#running-with-sse-or-streamable-http)

The server supports alternative transports for use with other MCP clients:

```
# SSE transport
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server --transport sse

# Streamable HTTP transport
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server --transport streamable-http
```

The default transport is `stdio`, which is used by Claude Desktop and Claude Code.

When running with `sse` or `streamable-http`, override the default bind address (`127.0.0.1:8000`) with the `--host` and `--port` flags:

```
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server --transport streamable-http --host 0.0.0.0 --port 7070
```

#### Running behind a reverse proxy or in Docker

[](#running-behind-a-reverse-proxy-or-in-docker)

The MCP SDK enables DNS-rebinding protection for the HTTP transports and, by default, only accepts `Host`/`Origin` headers for `localhost`. Behind a reverse proxy or in a container the client's `Host` header won't match, so requests fail with **`421 Misdirected Request`**.

Fix it by allow-listing the host(s) and origin(s) clients actually use (preferred over disabling protection). Values support `host`, `host:port`, and wildcard-port `host:*`:

```
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server --transport streamable-http --host 0.0.0.0 --port 7070 \
  --allowed-hosts ddg-mcp.example.com "ddg-mcp.example.com:*" \
  --allowed-origins "https://ddg-mcp.example.com"
```

Equivalent environment variables (comma-separated) are also available: `DDG_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, `DDG_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`.

As a last resort you can turn the check off entirely with `--disable-dns-rebinding-protection` (or `DDG_DISABLE_DNS_REBINDING_PROTECTION=1`). Prefer an allow-list — disabling protection removes a defense against DNS-rebinding attacks. When nothing is configured, the secure localhost-only default is preserved.

#### Running behind a TLS-intercepting proxy

[](#running-behind-a-tls-intercepting-proxy)

Corporate proxies that re-sign HTTPS traffic with their own CA (via `HTTPS_PROXY`) cause outbound requests to fail with certificate verification errors, because the HTTP clients don't trust the proxy's self-signed CA (and httpx no longer reads the `SSL_CERT_FILE` environment variable). Point the server at your proxy's CA bundle:

```
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server --ca-certs /path/to/proxy-ca.pem
```

Or set `DDG_CA_CERTS=/path/to/proxy-ca.pem`. The bundle is used by both the `search` and `fetch_content` tools, on the httpx and curl backends alike.

As a last resort, `--no-ssl-verify` (or `DDG_SSL_VERIFY=0`) disables certificate verification entirely. This exposes traffic to interception by anyone on the network path — prefer `--ca-certs`.

### Backends (bypassing bot detection)

[](#backends-bypassing-bot-detection)

Some sites — and, as of recently, DuckDuckGo's own search endpoint (`html.duckduckgo.com`) — block the default `httpx` client because of its distinctive TLS fingerprint, regardless of User-Agent. Cloudflare Bot Management and similar filters key on the JA3/TLS handshake, not on headers, so `html.duckduckgo.com` may answer `httpx` with an empty **HTTP 202** page (silently yielding "no results"). An opt-in backend, `curl` (implemented via `curl_cffi`), impersonates a real Chrome browser's TLS handshake and passes through those checks.

Both the `search` tool and the `fetch_content` tool support these backends.

**Installation:**

```
# Default install (httpx only)
uv pip install duckduckgo-mcp-server

# With the optional browser backend
uv pip install "duckduckgo-mcp-server[browser]"
```

**Backend options:**

| Value | Behavior | Needs `[browser]` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `httpx` | Lightweight async HTTP. Default. Works on most sites. | no |
| `curl` | Uses `curl_cffi` with Chrome 131 TLS impersonation. Passes TLS-fingerprint-based filters. | yes |
| `auto` | Tries `httpx` first; on 403 or a Cloudflare challenge response, retries with `curl`. | yes |

**Two ways to configure the backend:**

1. **Server-wide default** via the `--fetch-backend` CLI flag (applies to every `fetch_content` call):

```
# Default behavior — uses httpx
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server

# Force curl for every fetch (requires the [browser] extra)
uvx --with "duckduckgo-mcp-server[browser]" duckduckgo-mcp-server --fetch-backend curl

# Try httpx first, fall back to curl on 403 / Cloudflare challenge
uvx --with "duckduckgo-mcp-server[browser]" duckduckgo-mcp-server --fetch-backend auto
```

2. **Per-call override** via the `backend` argument on the `fetch_content` tool (overrides the CLI default for that single call). The tool exposes `backend` in its input schema, so an MCP client can choose `"httpx"`, `"curl"`, or `"auto"` on a fetch-by-fetch basis.

For `fetch_content`, the default stays `httpx` so users who don't need the impersonation don't pay for the extra dependency.

#### Search backend

[](#search-backend)

Because DuckDuckGo's search endpoint now fingerprint-blocks plain `httpx`, the `search` tool defaults to **`auto`**: it tries `httpx` first and falls back to `curl` when it detects a block (HTTP 202/403). The fallback only works if the `[browser]` extra is installed; otherwise search returns a message telling you to install it.

Configure the search backend with the `--search-backend` CLI flag or the `DDG_SEARCH_BACKEND` environment variable (`auto` (default) / `httpx` / `curl`):

```
# Recommended: install the browser extra so the auto fallback can impersonate Chrome
uvx --with "duckduckgo-mcp-server[browser]" duckduckgo-mcp-server

# Force curl for every search
uvx --with "duckduckgo-mcp-server[browser]" duckduckgo-mcp-server --search-backend curl

# Opt out of the fallback (legacy behavior — may return no results while blocked)
uvx duckduckgo-mcp-server --search-backend httpx
```

### Development

[](#development)

For local development:

```
# Install dependencies
uv sync

# Run with the MCP Inspector
mcp dev src/duckduckgo_mcp_server/server.py

# Install locally for testing with Claude Desktop
mcp install src/duckduckgo_mcp_server/server.py

# Run all tests
uv run python -m pytest src/duckduckgo_mcp_server/ -v

# Run only unit tests
uv run python -m pytest src/duckduckgo_mcp_server/test_server.py -v

# Run only e2e tests
uv run python -m pytest src/duckduckgo_mcp_server/test_e2e.py -v
```

## Available Tools

[](#available-tools)

### 1\. Search Tool

[](#1-search-tool)

```
async def search(query: str, max_results: int = 10, region: str = "") -> str
```

Performs a web search on DuckDuckGo and returns formatted results.

**Parameters:**

- `query`: Search query string

- `max_results`: Maximum number of results to return (default: 10)

- `region`: (Optional) Region/language code to override the default. Leave empty to use the configured default region.

**Region Code Examples:**

- `us-en`: United States (English)

- `cn-zh`: China (Chinese)

- `jp-ja`: Japan (Japanese)

- `de-de`: Germany (German)

- `fr-fr`: France (French)

- `wt-wt`: No specific region

**Returns:** Formatted string containing search results with titles, URLs, and snippets.

**Example Usage:**

- Search with default settings: `search("python tutorial")`

- Search with specific region: `search("latest news", region="jp-ja")` for Japanese news

### 2\. Content Fetching Tool

[](#2-content-fetching-tool)

```
async def fetch_content(
    url: str,
    start_index: int = 0,
    max_length: int = 8000,
    backend: Optional[str] = None,
    parse_mode: Optional[str] = None,
) -> str
```

Fetches and parses content from a webpage.

**Parameters:**

- `url`: The webpage URL to fetch content from, or a `ref://<id>` token from search results

- `start_index`: Character offset to start reading from (for pagination)

- `max_length`: Maximum number of characters to return

- `backend`: Optional per-call override of the default fetch backend (`"httpx"`, `"curl"`, or `"auto"`). When omitted, uses whatever was set via `--fetch-backend` at server startup.

- `parse_mode`: Optional per-call extractor (`"text"`, `"main"`, or `"markdown"`). When omitted, uses `DDG_PARSE_MODE` / `--parse-mode` (default `text`).

**Returns:** Cleaned and formatted text content from the webpage. The parsed full page is cached in memory (default 5 minutes) so later pages via `start_index` do not re-download. Metadata includes `cache=hit` or `cache=miss` when the cache is enabled.

> **SSRF protection:** By default `fetch_content` refuses URLs that resolve to loopback, private (RFC1918), link-local (including the `169.254.169.254` cloud metadata endpoint), reserved, multicast, or unspecified addresses, and it re-validates every redirect hop. Only `http`/`https` URLs are allowed. For trusted local deployments that need to fetch internal hosts, disable the guard with `DDG_ALLOW_PRIVATE_URLS=1` or `--allow-private-urls`. See [SECURITY.md](/nickclyde/duckduckgo-mcp-server/blob/main/SECURITY.md) for details.

### 3\. Link Expansion Tool

[](#3-link-expansion-tool)

```
async def expand_link(token: str) -> str
```

Search results replace URLs longer than `DDG_REF_URL_THRESHOLD` characters (default 120) with short, stable `ref://<id>` tokens so long tracking-laden links do not eat context. `fetch_content` accepts a token in place of a URL, so the model only needs this tool when it has to show or cite the real link.

**Parameters:**

- `token`: A `ref://<id>` token exactly as it appeared in search results (the bare id also works)

**Returns:** The full original URL, or an error if the token is unknown. Tokens live in memory for the lifetime of the server process (bounded by an LRU cap), so they are forgotten on restart.

## Features in Detail

[](#features-in-detail)

### Rate Limiting

[](#rate-limiting)

- Search: 30 requests per minute by default (`DDG_SEARCH_RPM` / `--search-rpm`)

- Content fetching: 20 requests per minute globally (`DDG_FETCH_RPM` / `--fetch-rpm`)

- Optional per-host fetch cap, off by default (`DDG_FETCH_HOST_RPM` / `--fetch-host-rpm`)

- Strategies: `sliding` (default) or `token_bucket` via `DDG_RATE_LIMIT_STRATEGY` / `--rate-limit-strategy`

- HTTP 429 responses honor `Retry-After` (capped at 30s) and retry once

- Cache hits on `fetch_content` skip both the download and the fetch rate limiter

### Content cache

[](#content-cache)

- In-memory TTL cache of the fully parsed page (before pagination)

- Default TTL 300 seconds, 64 entries, least-recently-used eviction

- Errors are never cached

- Configure with `DDG_CACHE_TTL` / `DDG_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES` or `--cache-ttl` / `--cache-max-entries`

- Set either value to `0` to disable

### Result Processing

[](#result-processing)

- Removes ads and irrelevant content

- Cleans up DuckDuckGo redirect URLs

- Formats results for optimal LLM consumption

- Truncates long content appropriately

### Content parsing modes

[](#content-parsing-modes)

`fetch_content` accepts `parse_mode`:

| Mode | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `text` | Historical default. Strip chrome, return flattened page text. |
| `main` | Keep the primary `article` / `main` / content container only. |
| `markdown` | Same primary content, rendered as lightweight markdown (headings, lists, links, code). |

### Content Safety

[](#content-safety)

- **SafeSearch Filtering**: Configured at server startup via `DDG_SAFE_SEARCH` environment variable

  - Controlled by administrators, not modifiable by AI assistants

  - Filters inappropriate content based on the selected level

  - Uses DuckDuckGo's official `kp` parameter

- **Region Localization**:

  - Default region set via `DDG_REGION` environment variable

  - Can be overridden per search request by AI assistants

  - Improves result relevance for specific geographic regions

### Error Handling

[](#error-handling)

- Comprehensive error catching and reporting

- Detailed logging through MCP context

- Graceful degradation on rate limits or timeouts

# Chrome DevTools for agents

[](#chrome-devtools-for-agents)

[![npm chrome-devtools-mcp package](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/363103cab760f0f0eb614b230b1d6ae09733c68cb47bbd23f096a5280e6a3a9a/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f6e706d2f762f6368726f6d652d646576746f6f6c732d6d63702e737667)](https://npmjs.org/package/chrome-devtools-mcp)

Chrome DevTools for agents (`chrome-devtools-mcp`) lets your coding agent (such as Antigravity, Claude, Cursor or Copilot) control and inspect a live Chrome browser. It is available as a Model-Context-Protocol (MCP) server and as a [CLI](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/cli.md). Both give your AI coding assistant access to the full power of Chrome DevTools for reliable automation, in-depth debugging, and performance analysis.

[Tool reference](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/tool-reference.md) \| [CLI](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/cli.md) \| [Skills](#skills) \| [Changelog](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md) \| [Contributing](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md) \| [Troubleshooting](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md) \| [Design Principles](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/design-principles.md)

## Key features

[](#key-features)

- **Get performance insights**: Uses [Chrome DevTools](https://github.com/ChromeDevTools/devtools-frontend) to record traces and extract actionable performance insights.

- **Advanced browser debugging**: Analyze network requests, take screenshots and check browser console messages (with source-mapped stack traces).

- **Reliable automation**. Uses [puppeteer](https://github.com/puppeteer/puppeteer) to automate actions in Chrome and automatically wait for action results.

## Disclaimers

[](#disclaimers)

`chrome-devtools-mcp` exposes content of the browser instance to the MCP clients allowing them to inspect, debug, and modify any data in the browser or DevTools. Avoid sharing sensitive or personal information that you don't want to share with MCP clients.

`chrome-devtools-mcp` officially supports Google Chrome and [Chrome for Testing](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/chrome-for-testing/) only. Other Chromium-based browsers may work, but this is not guaranteed, and you may encounter unexpected behavior. Use at your own discretion. We are committed to providing fixes and support for the latest version of [Extended Stable Chrome](https://chromiumdash.appspot.com/schedule).

Performance tools may send trace URLs to the Google CrUX API to fetch real-user experience data. This helps provide a holistic performance picture by presenting field data alongside lab data. This data is collected by the [Chrome User Experience Report (CrUX)](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/crux). To disable this, run with the `--no-performance-crux` flag.

## **Usage statistics**

[](#usage-statistics)

Google collects usage statistics (such as tool invocation success rates, latency, and environment information) to improve the reliability and performance of Chrome DevTools MCP.

Data collection is **enabled by default**. You can opt-out by passing the `--no-usage-statistics` flag when starting the server:

```
"args": ["-y", "chrome-devtools-mcp@latest", "--no-usage-statistics"]
```

Google handles this data in accordance with the [Google Privacy Policy](https://policies.google.com/privacy).

Google's collection of usage statistics for Chrome DevTools MCP is independent from the Chrome browser's usage statistics. Opting out of Chrome metrics does not automatically opt you out of this tool, and vice-versa.

Collection is disabled if `CHROME_DEVTOOLS_MCP_NO_USAGE_STATISTICS` or `CI` env variables are set.

## Update checks

[](#update-checks)

By default, the server periodically checks the npm registry for updates and logs a notification when a newer version is available. You can disable these update checks by setting the `CHROME_DEVTOOLS_MCP_NO_UPDATE_CHECKS` environment variable.

## Requirements

[](#requirements)

- [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) [LTS](https://github.com/nodejs/Release#release-schedule) version.

- [Chrome](https://www.google.com/chrome/) current stable version or newer.

- [npm](https://www.npmjs.com/)

## Getting started

[](#getting-started)

Add the following config to your MCP client:

```
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "chrome-devtools": {
      "command": "npx",
      "args": ["-y", "chrome-devtools-mcp@latest"]
    }
  }
}
```

Note

Using `chrome-devtools-mcp@latest` ensures that your MCP client will always use the latest version of the Chrome DevTools MCP server.

### MCP Client configuration

[](#mcp-client-configuration)

For setup instructions specific to your editor or agent (e.g. Antigravity, Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code), please see our [Client Configurations Guide](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/client-configurations.md).

### Your first prompt

[](#your-first-prompt)

Enter the following prompt in your MCP Client to check if everything is working:

```
Check the performance of https://developers.chrome.com
```

Your MCP client should open the browser and record a performance trace.

Note

The MCP server will start the browser automatically once the MCP client uses a tool that requires a running browser instance. Connecting to the Chrome DevTools MCP server on its own will not automatically start the browser.

## CLI

[](#cli)

The package also includes the `chrome-devtools` CLI. Use it to control the browser from your terminal without MCP:

```
npm i chrome-devtools-mcp@latest -g
chrome-devtools navigate_page 1 --url "https://developers.chrome.com"
chrome-devtools take_screenshot 1 --filePath screenshot.png
chrome-devtools stop
```

See the [CLI guide](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/cli.md) for all commands and options.

## Skills

[](#skills)

The package includes [skills](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills) that teach your agent how to use Chrome DevTools:

| Skill | Use it to |
| --- | --- |
| [`chrome-devtools`](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills/chrome-devtools) | Debug and automate pages with the MCP tools. |
| [`chrome-devtools-cli`](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills/chrome-devtools-cli) | Run the CLI and write shell scripts with it. |
| [`a11y-debugging`](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills/a11y-debugging) | Audit and fix accessibility issues. |
| [`cookie-debugging`](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills/cookie-debugging) | Debug cookies, sessions, and authentication. |
| [`debug-optimize-lcp`](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills/debug-optimize-lcp) | Debug and improve Largest Contentful Paint. |
| [`memory-leak-debugging`](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills/memory-leak-debugging) | Find and fix memory leaks. |
| [`troubleshooting`](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/skills/troubleshooting) | Fix connection problems between MCP and Chrome. |

### Install skills as a plugin

[](#install-skills-as-a-plugin)

A plugin installs the MCP server and all skills, including the CLI skill:

- **Claude Code:** Run `/plugin marketplace add ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp`, then `/plugin install chrome-devtools-mcp@chrome-devtools-plugins`.

- **Copilot / VS Code:** Run **Chat: Install Plugin From Source** and enter `ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp`.

- **Gemini CLI:** Run `gemini extensions install --auto-update https://github.com/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp`.

See the [Client Configurations Guide](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/client-configurations.md) for more information.

### Install skills manually

[](#install-skills-manually)

If your agent does not support plugins, copy the skill folders into the skills directory of your agent. The npm package contains the skills:

```
npm i chrome-devtools-mcp@latest -g
# Example for Claude Code. Use the skills directory of your agent.
mkdir -p ~/.claude/skills
cp -R "$(npm root -g)/chrome-devtools-mcp/skills/chrome-devtools-cli" ~/.claude/skills/
```

Important

The `chrome-devtools-cli` skill requires the CLI. Install the package globally before you use the skill.

### Keep skills up to date

[](#keep-skills-up-to-date)

Skills describe the tools of a specific version. Update the skills each time you update the MCP server or the CLI. Outdated skills can tell your agent to use tools or parameters that changed.

- **Plugins:** Use the update command of the plugin manager of your agent. The Gemini CLI command above enables automatic updates with `--auto-update`.

- **Manual installs:** Update the package, then copy the skill folders again:

```
npm i chrome-devtools-mcp@latest -g
mkdir -p ~/.claude/skills
cp -R "$(npm root -g)/chrome-devtools-mcp/skills/chrome-devtools-cli" ~/.claude/skills/
```

## Tools

[](#tools)

If you run into any issues, checkout our [troubleshooting guide](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md). See the full [Tool Reference](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/tool-reference.md) for a complete list of all supported MCP capabilities.

## Configuration

[](#configuration)

Find the complete list of server parameters (e.g., `--headless`, `--isolated`, `--slim`) and how to configure WebSocket connections in the [Configuration Guide](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/configuration.md).

For basic browser tasks only, the `--slim` flag exposes a reduced set of tools. See the [Slim tool reference](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/slim-tool-reference.md).

## Advanced Usage

[](#advanced-usage)

For advanced features such as handling concurrent sessions, persistent user data directories, connecting to a running Chrome instance instead of starting a new one, or debugging on Android, see our [Advanced Usage Guide](/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp/blob/main/docs/advanced-usage.md).

## Integrating as a browser subagent

[](#integrating-as-a-browser-subagent)

If you are developing agentic tooling and want to provide an integrated browser subagent as part of your product, we recommend building on top of Chrome DevTools for agents.

For a reference implementation, see the [Gemini CLI browser agent documentation](https://geminicli.com/docs/core/subagents/#browser-agent).

I'll use that form.
- **chrome-devtools-mcp**: `npx -y chrome-devtools-mcp@latest` — local, free, uses installed Chrome ✓ (Chrome stable present). Add `--no-usage-statistics` (privacy, optional — Google collects usage stats by default; I'll include the opt-out flag since it's cost-free).
- **Playwright MCP**: `bunx @playwright/mcp` (bun installed ✓); with Chrome installed, could use `--browser chrome`. Hmm — Playwright MCP `--browser` flag accepts browser names like `chrome` (channel). That avoids the ~120MB Chromium download. I'll use `["bunx", "@playwright/mcp", "--browser", "chrome"]`.
- **GitHub MCP**: official local = Docker `ghcr.io/github/github-mcp-server` — docker is installed ✓ but requires (a) Docker Desktop daemon running, (b) a GitHub PAT in env. `gh auth status` says NOT logged in. So GitHub MCP needs a PAT the user must create. Options: include with `{env:GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN}` and document the requirement; if env unset, server starts but tools fail — acceptable, documented. Docker Desktop might not be running (docker CLI exists; daemon status unknown — `docker info` check? Skip; note in plan that Docker Desktop must be running when GitHub MCP is used, and OpenCode will just fail that server's tools otherwise — actually if the command fails to start, OpenCode logs it and continues; other servers unaffected).

Include it; flag the PAT prerequisite as a user to-do (create fine-grained PAT, `export GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN=...` in shell profile or put in opencode env? OpenCode supports env in command strings via {env:VAR}. The server needs the env var visible to its process — passing `-e GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN={env:GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN}` in the docker args works with OpenCode substitution).

Also `gh` not authenticated — worth noting: without gh auth, native git push (SSH remote git@github.com:blalmal10a/io.git) works via SSH keys presumably. Fine.

Caveats to note in plan:
- Docker Desktop must be running for GitHub MCP; if not running, only that server fails.
- User must create a GitHub PAT (classic or fine-grained, repo scope) and set `GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` env var (e.g., in ~/.zshrc) — because gh isn't authenticated.
- Redundancy note: built-in websearch + webfetch exist; DDG MCP adds search results formatting/fetch; user asked for it explicitly. Fine.

```jsonc

Wait, for GitHub MCP passing the token: `-e GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` passes host env through — if the env var is set in the host shell that launches OpenCode, docker inherits it. That works without config substitution IF OpenCode's spawned processes inherit the user's shell env. OpenCode server process env — inherits from launch context. If launched from terminal with the var exported in .zshrc, fine. Explicit substitution `-e GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN={env:GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN}` would substitute empty string if unset — `-e VAR=` sets empty var, same failure mode. Documented as fragility to preserve, not a bug.

- All MCP launchers are installed and free/local: `bun`, `npx`, `uvx`, Docker, Google Chrome.

## `opencode.json` (final, complete)

```jsonc
{
  "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
  "instructions": [
    "ARCHITECTURE.md",
    "PLAN.md",
    "tasks/current_task-*.md"
  ],
  "permissions": [
    { "action": "shell", "resource": "*", "effect": "ask" },
    { "action": "external_directory", "resource": "*", "effect": "allow" },
    { "action": "edit", "resource": "*", "effect": "allow" },
    { "action": "edit", "resource": "/*", "effect": "deny" }
  ],
  "mcp": {
    "servers": {
      "playwright": {
        "type": "local",
        "command": ["bunx", "@playwright/mcp", "--browser", "chrome"]
      },
      "chrome-devtools": {
        "type": "local",
        "command": ["npx", "-y", "chrome-devtools-mcp@latest", "--no-usage-statistics"]
      },
      "context7": {
        "type": "local",
        "command": ["npx", "-y", "@upstash/context7-mcp"]
      },
      "ddg-search": {
        "type": "local",
        "command": ["uvx", "--with", "duckduckgo-mcp-server[browser]", "duckduckgo-mcp-server"]
      },
      "github": {
        "type": "local",
        "command": [
          "docker", "run", "-i", "--rm",
          "-e", "GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN",
          "ghcr.io/github/github-mcp-server"
        ]
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Why each choice:
- **playwright** — click-tests the MCQ SPA at `io.test`; `--browser chrome` reuses your installed Chrome (no 120 MB Chromium download).
- **chrome-devtools** — network/console debugging (would catch fetch/JS breakage classes); official Google server, usage statistics opted out.
- **context7** — current docs on demand (Firebase Hosting config, Alpine.js).
- **ddg-search** — `uvx` local stdio; the `[browser]` extra is included because DuckDuckGo now fingerprint-blocks plain HTTP clients (server's own README recommendation).
- **github** — official local Docker image; runs 100% locally, your repo remote (`blalmal10a/io`) already exists.

## Your one prerequisite (user action, not agent)

GitHub MCP needs a token: create a fine-grained PAT at github.com → Settings → Developer settings, then add to `~/.zshrc`:
`export GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN=ghp_...` (passed through to Docker via `-e`). Until then, only the `github` server fails; the rest work. Docker Desktop must also be running when that server is used.

## Purpose

Static study portal for the **Mizoram ICT Department Informatics Officer (Level-10) recruitment exam**: syllabus-structured study notes plus an MCQ practice tester. Hand-maintained HTML/CSS/JS — no build step, package manager, CI, or lint.

## Technical definition

- **Serve:** already running via Laravel Herd — do NOT start ad-hoc servers (no `python -m http.server`, no `npx serve`). Check with `curl`:
  - `curl -s io.test` — homepage
  - `curl -sI io.test/data/questions.json` — MCQ data (expect `200`, `application/json`)
- **Verify:** no test tooling exists. Verify by curling changed pages and, for browser behavior (MCQ tester JS), using the Playwright/chrome-devtools MCP against `io.test`. Validate JSON with `jq . public/data/questions.json`.
- **Layout:** top-level pages in `public/*.html` (`index.html`, `mcq.html`); one notes page per syllabus topic in `public/notes/*.html`; shared stylesheet `public/assets/style.css`; MCQ data `public/data/questions.json`; source of truth `public/syllabus.md` (parsed from the PDF next to it).
- **Git:** remote `github.com/blalmal10a/io` (SSH). `gh` is installed but unauthenticated.

History is append-only.

**ARCHITECTURE.md** content (detailed):

```markdown
# ARCHITECTURE.md

Technical structure of the IO Preparation Hub. Update this in the same session whenever the structure changes.

## Overview

Pure client-side static site. No backend, no build step, no framework. Pages are hand-written HTML that share one stylesheet; the only runtime data is `data/questions.json`, fetched by the MCQ tester.

## Serving (verified)

- **Herd** serves this repo with **`public/` as the document root**: `http://io.test` → `public/index.html`.
- All runtime asset URLs assume this. In particular `mcq.html` fetches `../data/questions.json`, which resolves to `/data/questions.json` — correct **only while `public/` is the docroot**. Serving from the repo root (or any other layout) breaks the MCQ data load.
- Planned production: Firebase Hosting, `firebase.json` with `"public": "public"` — preserves the same paths. Not yet configured; no `firebase.json` exists.

## Directory layout

```
public/
├── index.html                     # Home hub: topic links, quick links
├── mcq.html                       # MCQ Tester — inline vanilla-JS SPA
├── syllabus.md                    # Parsed syllabus (source of truth)
├── posts-286-notified-syllabus-for-direct-recruitment-to-the-post-of-io.pdf
├── assets/
│   └── style.css                  # Single shared stylesheet, all classes
├── data/
│   └── questions.json             # MCQ bank, fetched at runtime
└── notes/
    ├── index.html                 # Notes TOC (papers → topics)
    └── <topic>.html               # 14 topic pages (scaffolds today)
```

## Page depths & relative paths

Two depths; links differ:
- `public/*.html` → `assets/style.css`, `notes/index.html`, `data/...`
- `public/notes/*.html` → `../assets/style.css`, `index.html`, `../mcq.html`

When adding pages, copy the depth's existing link style.

## Syllabus structure (from `public/syllabus.md`)

Written exam 800 marks (3h/paper) + interview 100 = 900 total.

| Paper | Marks |
|---|---|
| General English | 100 |
| General Studies | 100 |
| Technical Subject Paper-I | 200 (Discrete Math 40, Computer Architecture 40, DSA 60, OS 60) |
| Technical Subject Paper-II | 200 (OOP 40, Web Tech 60, DBMS 60, Cloud Computing 40) |
| Technical Subject Paper-III | 200 (Networks 60, Software Engineering 60, AI/ML 40, Cybersecurity 40) |

## Notes page anatomy (scaffold)

Each topic page follows `public/notes/dbms.html`:
- `header.site` nav + `.breadcrumbs`
- `.banner.info` with Phase-1 status
- `h3` Modules outline as `ul.checklist` (syllabus subtopics verbatim)
- `.placeholder` blocks for unprompted content
- Footer citing the syllabus source

Population ("populate [Topic Name]") replaces scaffold placeholders with syllabus-aligned notes; see PLAN.md for rules.

## MCQ tester (`public/mcq.html`)

Single inline `<script>`, vanilla JS, no build:
- State: `currentPaper`, `currentQuestionIndex`, `selectedOption`, `score`, etc.
- `loadQuestions()` `fetch()`es `../data/questions.json` and replaces the hardcoded fallback array (13 questions with overlapping ids).
- Rendering via `innerHTML` template + inline `onclick` handlers → helper functions must stay global (script must remain classic, **not** `type="module"`).
- Keyboard: ←/→ = prev/next. Hash routing present but broken (see defects). Settings/localStorage advertised in copy but not implemented.

### questions.json schema

```json
{
  "id": 1,
  "paper": "General Studies",   // syllabus topic name
  "topic": "Geography",
  "question": "…",
  "options": ["…", "…", "…", "…"],
  "answer": 0,                   // 0-BASED index into options
  "explanation": "…"
}
```

Scoring convention (exam-style): **2 marks per correct, −1/3 per wrong** (stated in site copy; the implemented score math is a placeholder, see defects).

## Known defects / quirks (do not silently "fix")

1. `fetch("../data/questions.json")` — works only because docroot is `public/`. Fragile by design; keep as-is.
2. Alpine.js loaded from unpkg (`https://unpkg.com/alpinejs@3.x.x/dist/alpine.js`) but never used.
3. Score logic placeholder: `submitAnswer()` uses `questions.findIndex(q => q.answer === selectedOption)` (matches first question with that answer index, not the current question) and `renderResult()` percentage math is wrong (`totalCorrect = (totalQuestions - currentQuestionIndex) + (currentQuestionIndex + 1)`).
4. Hash-change handler references `document.hash.name` (nonexistent) → throws; `#quiz/#settings/#result` routing doesn't work.
5. `mcq.html` nav links all point to `index.html` (placeholders); filtering by paper is not implemented (shows all questions regardless of `currentPaper`).

## Styling

One stylesheet: `public/assets/style.css` (CSS variables in `:root`; utility vocabulary). Reuse existing classes — `card`, `banner info/warn`, `checklist`, `placeholder`, `toc`, `breadcrumbs`, `badge`, `btn`, `q-card`, `option`, `explanation`, `review-item`, `grid-2`, `row`, `tabs`, `muted`, `small`, `section-title` — instead of adding per-page CSS.

## Deployment

Target: Firebase Hosting (`public/` as site root), not yet set up. Local GitHub remote exists (`git@github.com:blalmal10a/io.git`). `gh` CLI installed but unauthenticated.
```

Wait — defect #3 percentage: let me double check `renderResult`: `totalCorrect = (totalQuestions - currentQuestionIndex) + (currentQuestionIndex + 1)` — that's totalQuestions+1 always. Percentage = ((totalQuestions+1)/totalQuestions)*100. Wrong math, yes.

Also defect: fetch happens async in `loadQuestions()` but `totalQuestions` was captured from hardcoded array at parse time and is never updated after fetch → after fetch replaces `questions`, `totalQuestions` stays 13 even if JSON has more. Add to defects: "`totalQuestions` is captured from the hardcoded array at load and never recomputed after the async fetch". Actually `totalQuestions = questions.length` at line 181, `loadQuestions` reassigns `questions` but not `totalQuestions`. So the tester shows "Question X of 13" regardless. Correct defect list. Also `navigateToPaper` calls `loadQuestions()` (async) then `renderQuiz()` immediately — race: renders before fetch resolves. That's a race condition defect. I'll fold: "async fetch races with synchronous render; `totalQuestions` never updated after fetch".

Don't over-detail; keep defect list sharp but complete enough for agents.

**PLAN.md**:

```markdown
# PLAN.md — hard rules for agents

These rules were agreed with the user. Never violate them; ask first if a task seems to require it.

1. **No ad-hoc servers.** The site is served by Laravel Herd at `http://io.test` with `public/` as document root. Never start `python -m http.server`, `npx serve`, or similar.
2. **`public/` is always the docroot.** Relative paths (including `mcq.html`'s `../data/questions.json`) depend on it. Never reorganize `public/` without updating all links and the fetch path — and updating `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
3. **Topic-by-topic content.** Notes and MCQs are built one topic at a time, after discussing with the user. Never mass-populate multiple topics unprompted. Content must be syllabus-aligned (source: `public/syllabus.md`).
4. **Syllabus is frozen.** Use the topic list and mark weights from `public/syllabus.md`. Do not restructure papers/topics or invent mark weights.
5. **MCQ data integrity.** Preserve the `questions.json` schema exactly (`answer` is a 0-based index; `paper` uses the syllabus topic name). 2 marks per question, 1/3 negative marking convention.
6. **No wholesale `mcq.html` rewrites.** Small verified bugs may be fixed on sight and flagged in the response; larger changes need the user's go-ahead.
7. **Write only inside this project directory.** Reads anywhere are allowed; edits/writes outside the repo are not.
8. **Daily task roll is mandatory** on date change (see AGENTS.md). `tasks/history/` is append-only — never edit or delete archived task files.
9. **Keep docs truthful.** When structure changes, update `ARCHITECTURE.md` in the same session. Never document something unverified.
```

Pages are hand-written HTML sharing one stylesheet; the only runtime data is `data/questions.json`, fetched by the MCQ tester.\n\n## Serving (verified)\n\n- **Laravel Herd** serves this repo with **`public/` as the document root**: `http://io.test` → `public/index.html` (nginx, PHP 8.4 proxy).\n- All runtime asset URLs assume this. In particular `mcq.html` fetches `../data/questions.json`, which resolves to `/data/questions.json` — correct **only while `public/` is the docroot**. Serving from the repo root would break the MCQ data load.\n- Planned production: Firebase Hosting with `firebase.json` → `\"public\": \"public\"`, preserving the same paths. Not yet configured; no `firebase.json` exists.\n\n## Directory layout\n\n```text\npublic/\n├── index.html                 # Home hub: topic links, quick links\n├── mcq.html                   # MCQ Tester — inline vanilla-JS SPA\n├── syllabus.md                # Parsed syllabus (source of truth)\n├── posts-286-notified-syllabus-for-direct-recruitment-to-the-post-of-io.pdf\n├── assets/\n│   └── style.css              # Single shared stylesheet (all classes)\n├── data/\n│   └── questions.json         # MCQ bank, fetched at runtime\n└── notes/\n    ├── index.html             # Notes TOC (papers → topics)\n    └── <topic>.html           # 14 topic pages (scaffolds today)\n```\n\n## Page depths & relative paths\n\nTwo depths; link prefixes differ:\n\n- `public/*.html` → `assets/style.css`, `notes/index.html`, `data/…`\n- `public/notes/*.html` → `../assets/style.css`, `index.html`, `../mcq.html`\n\nWhen adding pages, copy the link style of the existing page at that depth.\n\n## Syllabus structure (from `public/syllabus.md`)\n\nWritten exam 800 marks (3 h per paper) + personal interview 100 = 900 total.\n\n| Paper | Marks | Topics |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| General English | 100 | — |\n| General Studies | 100 | — |\n| Technical Subject Paper-I | 200 | Discrete Math 40, Computer Architecture 40, DSA 60, Operating Systems 60 |\n| Technical Subject Paper-II | 200 | OOP 40, Web Technologies 60, DBMS 60, Cloud Computing 40 |\n| Technical Subject Paper-III | 200 | Computer Networks 60, Software Engineering 60, AI/ML 40, Cybersecurity 40 |\n\n## Notes page anatomy (scaffold)\n\nEach topic page follows `public/notes/dbms.html`:\n\n- `header.site` nav + `.breadcrumbs` line\n- `.banner.info` with Phase-1 status\n- `h3` \"Modules (syllabus outline)\" as `ul.checklist` (syllabus subtopics)\n- `.placeholder` blocks marking unprompted content\n- Footer citing the syllabus source\n\nPopulation (\"populate [Topic Name]\", see PLAN.md) replaces scaffold placeholders with syllabus-aligned notes and adds matching MCQs to `data/questions.json`.\n\n## MCQ tester (`public/mcq.html`)\n\nSingle inline `<script>`, vanilla JS, no build:\n\n- State globals: `currentPaper`, `currentQuestionIndex`, `selectedOption`, `score`, `totalQuestions`, …\n- `loadQuestions()` `fetch()`es `../data/questions.json` and replaces the hardcoded fallback array (~13 questions with overlapping ids).\n- Rendering builds `innerHTML` templates with inline `onclick` handlers → helper functions must stay global; the script must remain a **classic script (not `type=\"module\"`)**.\n- Keyboard: ←/→ = previous/next question.\n- Hash routing (`#quiz` / `#settings` / `#result`) is advertised but broken (see defects). Settings/localStorage described in page copy are not implemented.\n\n### `questions.json` schema\n\n```json\n{\n  \"id\": 1,\n  \"paper\": \"General Studies\",\n  \"topic\": \"Geography\",\n  \"question\": \"…\",\n  \"options\": [\"…\", \"…\", \"…\", \"…\"],\n  \"answer\": 0,\n  \"explanation\": \"…\"\n}\n```\n\n- `answer` is a **0-based index** into `options`.\n- `paper` uses the syllabus topic name.\n- Scoring convention (exam-style): **2 marks per correct, −1/3 per wrong** (stated in site copy; the implemented math is a placeholder, see defects).\n\n## Known defects / quirks (do not silently \"fix\")\n\n1. `fetch(\"../data/questions.json\")` works only because the docroot is `public/`. Fragile by design; keep it and keep the docroot assumption.\n2. Alpine.js loaded from unpkg (`https://unpkg.com/alpinejs@3.x.x/dist/alpine.js`) but never used — no `x-*` attributes anywhere.\n3. Score logic is placeholder-quality: `submitAnswer()` uses `questions.findIndex(q => q.answer === selectedOption)` (matches the first question with that answer index, not the current question), and `renderResult()` computes `totalCorrect = (totalQuestions - currentQuestionIndex) + (currentQuestionIndex + 1)` (always `totalQuestions + 1`).\n4. The hash-change handler reads `document.hash.name` (nonexistent) → throws; `#quiz`/`#settings`/`#result` routing never fires.\n5. `totalQuestions` is captured from the hardcoded array at parse time and never recomputed after the async fetch; `navigateToPaper()` also renders synchronously before the fetch resolves (race).\n6. `mcq.html` nav links all point to `index.html` (placeholders), and questions are not filtered by `currentPaper`.\n\n## Styling\n\nOne stylesheet: `public/assets/style.css`. CSS variables in `:root` (`--bg`, `--card`, `--accent`, `--ok`, `--bad`, `--warn`, …). Reuse the existing class vocabulary — `card`, `banner info/warn`, `checklist`, `placeholder`, `toc`, `breadcrumbs`, `badge`, `btn`, `q-card`, `option`, `explanation`, `review-item`, `grid-2`, `row`, `tabs`, `muted`, `small`, `section-title` — instead of adding per-page CSS.\n\n## Deployment\n\nTarget: Firebase Hosting with `public` as site root (not set up). Git remote: `git@github.com:blalmal10a/io.git`. `gh` CLI installed but unauthenticated.\n"
}

Let me think through the trade-offs genuinely.

Arguments for keeping plain HTML/CSS/JS (status quo):
- The project is fundamentally **content**: 14 topic pages of study notes + a growing JSON question bank. Content is the bottleneck, not interactivity. Frameworks shine when state/interactivity complexity dominates; here the most interactive surface is one MCQ tester page, ~350 lines of vanilla JS.
- No build step = no dependency churn, no version drift, no node_modules, nothing to break. Agents can edit files with zero toolchain risk. For a project driven by AI agents over months, minimizing machinery is a real win: every added framework adds failure modes, config, and upgrade tasks that compete with the actual goal (exam content).
- Serving is already working (Herd → io.test), Firebase Hosting static deploy of `public/` is one file (firebase.json).
- Performance is inherently excellent (static pages, tiny CSS). No hydration, no JS framework payload.
- Longevity: exam prep content in plain HTML is readable/editable forever; no framework deprecation risk. This is a personal long-horizon project (exam date-driven).
- The MCQ tester's bugs are all small, localized, and fixable in vanilla JS. Rewriting in a framework wouldn't change the data model (JSON bank + render + score).

Arguments for a lightweight framework (SPA):
- If the MCQ tester grows: multiple papers/subtopics with filters, timers, adaptive practice, review modes, localStorage state, maybe spaced repetition — componentization (e.g., Preact/Alpine/Svelte/Vue/React) would make that manageable. Also shared components across pages (nav, breadcrumbs) avoid hand-copying headers in 16 HTML files — a real current pain: adding a page requires manually updating nav links in every other page (no templating!). That's the strongest genuine argument FOR a framework or at least a static site generator (SSG).
- Also per-paper question counts, search across notes, dark mode, etc. — easier with a framework.

Middle ground options worth presenting:
1. **Keep static, stay hand-rolled** — recommended for now.

Firebase. Astro is particularly apt: content collections (markdown for notes!), MDX, zero-JS-by-default, easy to add interactive islands for the MCQ tester. At that point, **Astro** (best content fit, markdown notes, islands) or **Eleventy** (simplest) — deployed to Firebase Hosting as a static site (build output dir, e.g., `dist`). Not an SPA: there's no reason for client-side routing for a notes portal; static output keeps Firebase Hosting + Herd serving identical.

Answer structure:

**TL;DR:** Keep it static. If you ever adopt tooling, make it a static site generator (Astro/Eleventy), not an SPA framework. And if you do switch, do it soon — while notes are still empty scaffolds, migration is nearly free.

**Why static-now is right (default):**
- Your project is content-bound, not app-bound. The interactivity surface is one page (~350 lines vanilla JS). Frameworks pay off when state complexity dominates; here it doesn't.
- No build step = agents edit files directly, nothing to install/upgrade/break; your PLAN.md encodes this and the whole docs suite assumes it.
- Serves at native-static speed on Herd today, and drops onto Firebase Hosting with one file later.
- Plain HTML notes are permanent — no framework deprecation risk over a months-long study project.

**The genuine pains that eventually justify a switch:**
1. Shared markup: nav/breadcrumbs are copy-pasted in 16 pages; adding a page means hand-editing every other page's nav.
2. MCQ tester growth: paper/topic filtering, review mode, shuffle, settings/localStorage, score stats — all currently broken/absent.

**If/when you switch — what to pick:**
- **Astro** (best fit): notes as markdown content collections (nicer for agent-authored notes, cleaner diffs, more trustworthy than hand-written HTML), zero JS by default, MCQ tester as a single interactive island, build output is still a static site → Firebase Hosting unchanged, Herd can serve `dist/`.
- **Eleventy** if you want even less machinery.
- **Not an SPA** (React/Vue/Next client-routed): wrong shape for a documents portal — worse linkability/bookmarking, framework payload, full rewrite, and no compensating benefit.

**Deploy question:** "SPA or static?" is answered by content, not framework: this should deploy as a static site either way. Even the SPA option ends up static files on Firebase; the difference is client routing, which you don't need.

**Decision trigger / timing nuance:** If you think you'll ever want the tester features or markdown notes, migrating NOW (14 tiny scaffolds) is minutes; migrating after months of accumulated notes is a costly rewrite. ## Your question: framework vs. plain HTML, SPA vs. static?

**Short answer: stay static — and if you ever adopt tooling, make it a static site generator (Astro or Eleventy), not an SPA framework.**

**Why the current approach is actually the right default here:**

- Your project is **content-bound, not app-bound**. The real work is 14+ pages of study notes and a growing question bank. Frameworks pay off when interactive state dominates; here the entire interactive surface is one ~350-line vanilla-JS page.
- **No build step is an asset for agent-driven work.** Agents edit files directly — nothing to install, version-drift, or break, and the whole docs suite we just wrote encodes that. A framework makes every content session start with "did the toolchain change?"
- **Deployment is trivial either way** — one `firebase.json` when you're ready, served at native-static speed.
- Plain HTML notes never deprecate. Over a months-long study project, that longevity matters.

**The two genuine pains that would justify switching:**

1. **Shared markup** — header/nav/breadcrumbs are copy-pasted into 16 pages; adding a page means hand-editing every other page's nav.
2. **MCQ tester growth** — paper filtering, review mode, shuffle, settings, score stats are all missing or broken today.

**If you switch, pick Astro** (best fit for this exact project):

- Notes become **markdown content collections** — much cleaner for agent-authored notes than hand-written HTML (less markup noise, reviewable diffs).
- Zero JS by default; the MCQ tester becomes a single interactive island.
- Output is still a static site → Firebase Hosting plan unchanged; Herd can serve the built `dist/` so the dev workflow keeps `io.test`.
- Eleventy if you want even less machinery. **Not an SPA** (React/Vue client-routed) — wrong shape for a documents portal: worse bookmarking/link-sharing of study sections, a full rewrite, framework payload, and no compensating benefit.

**"SPA or static site" is decided by content, not framework:** this project should deploy as a *static site* in every scenario. The only real fork is "hand-written HTML vs. generator-built HTML."

**One timing nuance that makes this urgent-ish:** if a switch is likely at all, do it **now, while notes are empty scaffolds** — migrating 14 tiny pages takes minutes; migrating months of accumulated rich notes later is a costly, error-prone rewrite. | Paper/topic filtering, review mode, shuffle | The MCQ tester becomes one **island** with a proper state machine — the deferred defects (score logic, no filtering, dead-start UI, hash routing) get fixed by design in the rewrite |
| Exam timers | Per-paper 3-h timer + section timers, persisted in localStorage |
| Score stats | Per-topic accuracy history in localStorage + a small stats page |
| Cross-note search | Build-time generated search index (e.g. MiniSearch) — still fully static, works on Firebase |
| Notes authoring | Notes become **markdown files** — cleaner for agent-authored content, reviewable diffs |
| Data safety | `questions.json` moves to a **build-time import with schema validation** — wrong `answer` indexes, duplicate ids, or invented paper names fail the build instead of silently rendering wrong |
| Nav/TOC boilerplate | Generated from frontmatter (paper, marks, order) — the copy-pasted nav across 16 pages disappears |
| The fetch fragility | Disappears entirely — data is imported at build, not fetched at runtime |

## Proposed layout

```text
io/
├── package.json, astro.config.mjs      # build tooling (node already installed ✓)
├── firebase.json                       # later: "public": "dist"
├── src/
│   ├── layouts/Base.astro              # shared header/nav/footer (kills the copy-paste)
│   ├── styles/site.css                 # ported assets/style.css — same class vocabulary
│   ├── content/notes/                  # 14 markdown topic pages, frontmatter: paper/marks/order
│   ├── data/questions.json             # unchanged schema, now build-validated
│   ├── components/mcq/                 # tester island (vanilla JS module first)
│   └── pages/
│       ├── index.astro                 # home
│       ├── mcq.astro                   # mounts the island
│       └── notes/[slug].astro          # renders each markdown topic
└── dist/                               # build output (gitignored)
```

## Phases

1. **Checkpoint** — add `.gitignore` (`node_modules/`, `dist/`, `.astro/`, `.DS_Store`), commit current state.
2. **Scaffold + port** — Astro init, port `style.css` verbatim, `index.astro`, notes TOC (generated), 14 markdown scaffolds from the existing pages.
3. **Tester rewrite** — port MCQ tester as a vanilla-module island; fold in the fixed state bugs; add paper filtering + correct 2-marks/−⅓ scoring.
4. **Full-scope features** — timers, stats/localStorage, settings, search index.
5. **Cutover** — Herd docroot → `dist/`, delete legacy `public/*.html` (keep syllabus PDF + source), browser-verify everything via Playwright.
6. **Docs suite refresh** — `AGENTS.md` (new commands: `npm run dev/build`), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (new structure), `PLAN.md` (rules 1, 2, 6 rewritten for a build-based workflow), `opencode.json` adds `watcher.ignore` for `dist/**`/`node_modules/**`.
7. **Firebase** — when you create a Firebase project: `firebase.json` + first deploy (exact config verified via context7 at that time).

**Sequencing note:** topic population moves to *after* migration — populating in markdown is strictly better, and the current scaffolds make migration nearly free today vs. expensive later.

**User actions (carried over):** GitHub PAT + Docker Desktop for the github MCP; later, a Firebase project.

Hand-writing avoids scaffold interactivity in a non-empty repo. Astro minimal requirements: package.json with astro dependency, astro.config.mjs (optional actually — Astro works with zero config), tsconfig extending astro/tsconfigs/base. And content collections in Astro v5 use `src/content.config.ts` with glob loader:

```ts
import { defineCollection, z } from 'astro:content';
import { glob } from 'astro/loaders';

const notes = defineCollection({
  loader: glob({ pattern: '**/*.md', base: './src/content/notes' }),
  schema: z.object({
    title: z.string(),
    paper: z.string(),
    marks: z.number(),
    order: z.number(),
  }),
});

export const collections = { notes };
```

Astro v5 content layer API: `glob` loader from 'astro/loaders', file lives at `src/content.config.ts` (new location) or `src/content/config.ts` (legacy but still supported). In v5, `src/content.config.ts` is the standard. Rendering: `render(entry)` from 'astro:content' (v5: `const { Content } = await render(entry);`).
